is large enough so that
\lambda_{n}\geq 1
. We will also assume that
n\in\mathbb{N}
is large enough that

\displaystyle\nabla\mu_{t^{*}+h}^{\prime}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1}\nabla\mu_{t^{*}+h}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}\Lambda^{-1}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}h,

(86)

\displaystyle\mu_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}+h}

\displaystyle\leq 1/2\leq\sqrt{1/3}\cdot\Delta_{n},

for all
h\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{n})
.

Lower truncation term.

If
u\geq\mu_{t^{*}}
then
\bar{u}_{t^{*}}=u,\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\Delta_{n}<u
and so the lower truncation term is
0
. Otherwise
u<\mu_{t^{*}},\bar{u}_{t^{*}}=\mu_{t^{*}},\bar{H}_{t^{*}}=-\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}
. We apply the upper bound (
85
) on the determinant term – noting that
y<\bar{u}_{t^{*}}
for all
y\in\mathcal{I}_{-}^{c}
– and use the bounds from (
86
) to upper bound the density of the height and gradient, giving

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\mathcal{I}_{-}^{c}}\rho(t,y)\,dt\,dy

\displaystyle\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\int_{B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{n})}\int_{-\infty}^{\mu_{t^{*}}-\Delta_{n}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}}+\sqrt{1/3}\cdot\Delta_{n})^{2}\Big)\cdot\exp(-\frac{1}{4}h^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}h)\,dy\,dh

\displaystyle\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\int_{-\infty}^{\mu_{t^{*}}-\Delta_{n}}f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\Phi\Big(-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\Delta_{n}\Big)\int_{B(0,\varepsilon_{n})}\exp(-\frac{1}{4}h^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}h)\,dh

\displaystyle\leq C\Phi\Big(-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\Delta_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}.

with the final inequality following by Mills’ inequality (
144
) and the definition of
\Delta_{n}
. Finally, since
\mu_{t^{*}}>u
it can be verified that
\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\geq c>0
, so that the lower truncation term is at most
C\cdot\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\cdot\delta_{n}^{2}
.

Upper truncation term.

For the upper truncation term, applying (
85
) and integrating over
y\in[\bar{u}_{t^{*}}+\Delta_{n},\infty)
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\mathcal{I}_{+}^{c}}\rho(t,y)\,dt\,dy

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\bar{u}_{t^{*}}+\Delta_{n}}^{\infty}(\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})+(y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}})^{d})\cdot f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\bar{u}_{t^{*}}+\Delta_{n}}^{\infty}(\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})+\Delta_{n}^{d}+(y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\Delta_{n})^{d})\cdot f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}C(\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})+\Delta_{n}^{d})\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\Psi(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t}+\Delta_{n})\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\Psi(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t}+\Delta_{n})\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dt.

where
(i)
relies on the bound on Truncated Gaussian moments in (
146
). If
\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}\not\to\infty
, then the remaining argument is very similar to the analysis of the lower truncation term, and so we focus on the case where
\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}\to\infty
. Applying the upper bound (
144
) on Mills’ ratio again:

\displaystyle\Psi(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t}+\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{n}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t})^{2})}{\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t}+\Delta_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{n}^{2}\Big)\frac{\exp\big(-\frac{1}{2}(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\big)}{\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{n}^{2}\Big)\Psi(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}).

Plugging this into our previous bound on the upper truncation term, using the bound from (
86
) to upper bound
f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)
, and integrating over
t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
, we conclude that the upper truncation error is at most

C\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{n}^{2}\Big)\Psi(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}})\leq C\cdot\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{n}^{2}\Big)\leq C\cdot\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\cdot\delta_{n}^{3}.

10.8
Proof of Lemma
8

Recall that
\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)
is defined in (
20
). Let

\tilde{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y):=\frac{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\Big(1+T_{01}^{\rho}(y)+T_{21}^{\rho}(h,y)\Big)}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d+1}\det(\Lambda_{t^{*}})}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}}{2}\Big)\cdot\exp\bigg(-\frac{h^{\prime}\bar{G}_{t^{*}}h}{2}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{1}(y>u).

Note that the only difference between
\tilde{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)
and
\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)
is that the former omits the first-order terms
T_{10}^{\rho}(h)
and
T_{30}^{\rho}(h)
. We write
\rho(y)
in terms of a main term and three error terms:

\rho(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)\,dh-\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\tilde{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)\,dh+\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\tilde{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)-\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)\,dh+\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\rho(t^{*}+h,y)-\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)\,dh.

The second of the three error terms is zero,

\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\tilde{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)\,dh,

because
T_{10}^{\rho}(h),T_{30}^{\rho}(h)
are odd functions of
h
and
\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
is symmetric about the origin. We proceed to bound the relative error between the main term and
\bar{\rho}(y)
, and then bound the magnitude of the remaining two error terms. In doing so, we will at times use the fact that the first-order terms in the definition of
\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)
are indeed asymptotically vanishing, as

\displaystyle T_{01}^{\rho}(y)

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{n}\delta_{n}\leq C\varepsilon_{n}

\displaystyle T_{10}^{\rho}(h)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon_{n}

\displaystyle T_{30}^{\rho}(h)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}\{\log(\lambda_{n})\}^{3/2}\leq C\varepsilon_{n}\log(\lambda_{n})

\displaystyle T_{21}^{\rho}(h)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}\{\log(\lambda_{n})\}^{3/2}\leq C\varepsilon_{n}\log(\lambda_{n}).

We assume
n\in\mathbb{N}
is large enough that
C\varepsilon_{n}\log(\lambda_{n})\leq 1/2
.

Main term.

Direct computation shows that the main term is

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)\,dh

\displaystyle=\Big(1+T_{01}^{\rho}(y)+\frac{(y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}})}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{G}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}})\Big)\cdot\frac{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})}{\sqrt{\det(\bar{G}_{t^{*}}\Lambda_{t^{*}})}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\Big(-\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}}{2}\Big)

(87)

\displaystyle=\Big(1+\frac{1}{2}T_{01}^{\rho}(y)\Big)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})}{\det(-\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}})}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\Big(-\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}}{2}\Big)=:\tilde{\rho}(y).

with the second equality following upon recalling that
\bar{G}_{t^{*}}=-\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1}\bar{H}_{t^{*}}
.

We have shown that the main term
\tilde{\rho}(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)\,dh
is a valid first-order expansion of
\rho(y)
about
y=\bar{u}_{t^{*}}
. We now show that this expansion can be rewritten as a Gaussian density with a first-order shift in the mean, at the cost of another second-order error term.
Assuming
n\in\mathbb{N}
is large enough that
C\varepsilon_{n}<1/2
, it follows from first-order Taylor expansion that
\sup_{x\leq C\varepsilon_{n}}|1+x-\exp(x)|\leq 2x^{2}
. Plugging in
x=\frac{1}{2}T_{01}^{\rho}(y)
, we conclude that

\bigg|\Big(1+\frac{1}{2}T_{01}^{\rho}(y)\Big)-\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})\cdot\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}})-\frac{1}{2}(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}})\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}})\Big)\bigg|\leq C\Big((y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}})^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

Multiplying the second term inside the absolute value by
\exp(-\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}})^{2})
costs only an additional factor of
C\delta_{n}^{2}
. Plugging the result back into (
87
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{\rho}(y)-\bar{\rho}(y)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\sqrt{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)\det(-\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}})}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\Big)\Big((y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}})^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}\Big)

(88)

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\rho}(y)\cdot\Big((y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}})^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

Error due to local expansion.

The error due to integrating the local expansion
\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)
rather than
\rho(t^{*}+h,y)
is at most

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)-\rho(t^{*}+h,y)\,dh

(89)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(h,y)\cdot\bar{\rho}(h,y)\,dh

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\Big)\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(h,y)\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}\bar{G}_{t^{*}}h\Big)\cdot\,dh

\displaystyle\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\Big)\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{\det}(h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{Y}(h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{\nabla Y}(h,y)\Big)\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}\bar{G}_{t^{*}}h\Big)\,dh

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}C\frac{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})}{\sqrt{\det(\bar{G}_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\Big)\Big(|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|\delta_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}C\bar{\rho}(y)\Big(|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|\delta_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

In obtaining
(i)
we have applied the upper bound
\bar{\rho}(t,y)\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\exp(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G(y)h)\cdot\exp(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2})
; in
(ii)
we have integrated each component of
\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(t,y)
, and in
(iii)
we have applied the upper bound

\frac{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})}{\sqrt{\det(\bar{G}_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\Big)\leq C\bar{\rho}(y).

Error due to truncation.

In bounding the truncation error, observe that
\varepsilon_{n}\geq\{\lambda_{\min}(\bar{G}_{t^{*}})\}^{-1}\sqrt{6\log\lambda_{n}}
. Therefore,

\sqrt{\det(\bar{G}_{t^{*}})}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\exp\Big(-\frac{h^{\prime}\bar{G}_{t^{*}}h}{2}\Big)\,dh\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{d}(0,\sqrt{6\log\lambda_{n}})}\exp\Big(-\frac{\|z\|^{2}}{2}\Big)\,dz\leq C\lambda_{n}^{-2}=C\delta_{n}^{2},

(90)

the latter inequality following by (
150
). Similarly, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\det(\bar{G}_{t^{*}})}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\|h\|^{2}\exp\Big(-\frac{h^{\prime}\bar{G}_{t^{*}}h}{2}\Big)\,dh

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|h\|^{4}\phi_{0,\bar{G}_{t^{*}}^{-1}}(h)\,dt\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\phi_{0,\bar{G}_{t^{*}}^{-1}}(h)\,dt\Big)^{1/2}\leq C\delta_{n}^{4},

where we have written
\phi_{0,\bar{G}_{t^{*}}^{-1}}(h)
for the density of a
N_{d}(0,\bar{G}_{t^{*}}^{-1})
distribution.
The overall error due to truncation is thus at most

\displaystyle\frac{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})}{\sqrt{(2\pi)\det(\Lambda_{t^{*}}\bar{G}_{t^{*}})}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\Big)\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\Big(1+T_{01}^{\rho}(y)+T_{21}^{\rho}(h,y)\Big)\phi_{0,\bar{G}_{t^{*}}^{-1}}(h)\,dh

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\rho}(y)\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\Big(1+T_{01}^{\rho}(y)+T_{21}^{\rho}(h,y)\Big)\phi_{0,\bar{G}_{t^{*}}^{-1}}(h)\,dh

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\rho}(y)\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\Big(1+\|h\|^{2}\lambda_{\max}(-\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}})\Big)\phi_{0,\bar{G}_{t^{*}}^{-1}}(h)\,dh

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{\rho}(y)\delta_{n}^{2}.

This completes the proof.

10.9
Proof of Proposition
3

Throughout this proof we will assume that
n\in\mathbb{N}
is large enough that
\lambda_{n}\geq 1
. Our goal is to upper bound the expected number of peaks in the high-gradient region. An exact identity for this expectation is given by the Kac-Rice formula:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[N_{u}(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}})]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}}\int_{u}^{\infty}\rho(t,y)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}}\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}[\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})\cdot\mathbf{1}(\nabla^{2}Y_{t}\prec 0)|\nabla Y_{t}=0,Y_{t}=y]\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\cdot f_{Y_{t}}(y)\,dy\,dt.

Intuitively, this should be small relative to
\mathbb{E}[N_{u}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{n}})]
because
\|\mathbb{E}[\nabla Y_{t}]\|=\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|
is growing at all
t\in\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
. However, obtaining the correct bound requires careful handling of the determinant and height density terms above. The determinant term can be upper bounded using Assumption
8
, which upper bounds the curvature of the signal:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})\cdot\mathbf{1}(\nabla^{2}Y_{t}\prec 0)|\nabla Y_{t}=0,Y_{t}=y]

\displaystyle=\int\det(H_{t|y}+R)\cdot\mathbf{1}(H_{t|y}+R\succ 0)f_{R_{t}}(R)\,dR

\displaystyle\leq\int(\|H_{t|y}\|^{d}+\|R\|^{d})f_{R_{t}}(R)\,dR

\displaystyle\leq C(\det(H_{t|y})+1)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\nabla^{2}\mu_{t}\|^{d}+|y-\mu_{t}|^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d}+1)

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda_{n}^{d}+|y-\mu_{t}|^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d}).

Plugging this in to our exact expression for
\mathbb{E}[N_{u}(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}})]
and marginalizing over
y\in(u,\infty)
yields the upper bound

\mathbb{E}[N_{u}(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}})]\leq C\int_{\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}}(\lambda_{n}^{d}+(u-\mu_{t})_{+}^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\Psi(u-\mu_{t})f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dt,

(91)

where we have used (
146
) to upper bound
\int_{u}^{\infty}|y-\mu_{t}|^{d}f_{Y_{t}}(y)\,dy\leq C(1+(u-\mu_{t})_{+}^{d})\Psi(u-\mu_{t})
.

From here, we will apply Assumption
7
to upper bound the density of the gradient
f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)
. The precise nature of the analysis, and how it contributes to the ultimate upper bound, depends on how far
t
is from
T^{*}
. So we further partition
\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
into two subregions, and bound the expectation over each. By (
45
), there exist constants
\varepsilon_{0}>0,n_{0}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
,

\nabla\mu_{t^{*}+h}^{\prime}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1}\nabla\mu_{t^{*}+h}\geq\frac{3}{4}h^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}h,\quad\|\nabla\mu_{t^{*}+h}\|\leq 2\|\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}\|\|h\|,\quad 0\leq\mu_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}+h}\leq 1/2,

for all
t^{*}\in T^{*},h\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. We partition
\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
into
\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{0}
and
\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1}
, where
\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{0}
consists of points
t\in\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
that are within distance
\varepsilon_{0}
of
T^{*}
, while
\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1}
consists of all other points
t\in\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
:

\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{0}=\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\setminus\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{0}},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1}=\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cap\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{0}}.

(92)

By Assumption
7
, there exists a constant
n_{1}
such that
\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}\geq 6\sigma_{1}^{2}(\log|\mathcal{T}|+(d+2)\log\lambda_{n})
for all
n\geq n_{1}
. Moreover, there exists a constant
n_{2}
such that
3\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}-3d\sigma_{1}^{2}\log g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\geq\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}
for all
n\geq n_{2}
. Hereafter we will assume
n\geq\max(n_{0},n_{1},n_{2})
.

Upper bound on
\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{0})]
.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{t^{*}}:=B(t^{*},\varepsilon_{0})\setminus B(t^{*},\varepsilon_{n})
. For all
t\in\mathcal{G}_{t^{*}}
,
(\lambda_{n}^{d}+(u-\mu_{t})_{+}^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})
. Therefore, the expected number of points in
\mathcal{G}_{t^{*}}
is at most

\displaystyle C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\int_{\mathcal{G}_{t^{*}}}\Psi(u-\mu_{t})f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\cdot\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{*}})\cdot\int_{\mathcal{G}_{t^{*}}}f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\cdot\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{*}})\cdot\int_{B(0,\varepsilon_{0})\setminus B(0,\varepsilon_{n})}\exp(-\frac{3}{8}h^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1}\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}}h)\,dh

\displaystyle\leq C\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\cdot\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{*}})\cdot\int_{B(0,\varepsilon_{0})\setminus B(0,\varepsilon_{n})}\exp(-\frac{3\lambda_{n}^{2}}{8\sigma_{1}^{2}}\|h\|^{2})\,dh

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}C\cdot\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{*}})\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B(0,\sqrt{5\log\lambda_{n}})}\exp(-\frac{1}{2}\|z\|^{2})\,dz

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}C\cdot\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{*}})\cdot\{\log(\lambda_{n})\}^{d/2}\delta_{n}^{5/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{*}})\cdot\delta_{n}^{2},

where
(i)
follows by changing variables from
h
to
z=\sqrt{3\lambda_{n}/4\sigma_{1}^{2}}h
, and
(ii)
follows by a Chernoff bound as stated in (
150
).
Summing over
t^{*}\in T^{*}
, we conclude that
\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{0})]\leq C\sum_{t^{*}\in T^{*}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\delta_{n}^{2}
.

Upper bound on
\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1})]
.

To upper bound
\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1})]
, we will want to invoke Assumption
9
to upper bound the product of the determinant term and the Gaussian survival function in (
91
). Let
t^{**}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{t^{*}\in T^{*}}\mu_{t}
. At all points
t\in\mathcal{T}
such that
u-\mu_{t}<\sqrt{d}
,

(\lambda_{n}^{d}+(u-\mu_{t})_{+}^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\Psi(u-\mu_{t})\leq C(\lambda_{n}^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\Psi(u-\mu_{t})\leq C(\lambda_{n}^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\cdot\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{**}}),

(93)

with the last inequality following by Assumption
9
.
Otherwise if
u-\mu_{t}>\sqrt{d}
then
\mu_{t}\mapsto((u-\mu_{t})_{+}^{d}+\lambda_{n}^{d})\Psi(u-\mu_{t})
is monotonically increasing in
\mu_{t}
, and so for all such points
t\in\mathcal{T}

(\lambda_{n}^{d}+(u-\mu_{t})_{+}^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\Psi(u-\mu_{t})\leq C(\lambda_{n}^{d}+(u-\mu_{t^{**}})_{+}^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\cdot\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{**}}).

(94)

Therefore the upper bound in (
91
) is at most

C\int_{\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1}}(\lambda_{n}^{d}+(u-\mu_{t^{**}})_{+}^{d}+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{**}})f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dt\leq C\int_{\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1}}(\det(\bar{H}_{t^{**}})+\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d})\Psi(u-\mu_{t^{**}})f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dt.

(95)

We now apply Assumption
7
, which lower bounds
\inf_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1}}\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|\geq g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})
. As a result,

\inf_{t\in\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1}}\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{d}\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{1}^{2}}+d\log(\|\nabla\mu_{t}\|)\Big)\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{3\sigma_{1}^{2}}\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}\Big),

with the latter inequality following because
3\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}-3d\sigma_{1}^{2}\log g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\geq\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}
. As a result (
95
) is further upper bounded by

\displaystyle C\cdot|\mathcal{T}|\Psi\big(u-\mu_{t^{**}}\big)\det(\bar{H}_{t^{**}})\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{3\sigma_{1}^{2}}\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot|\mathcal{T}|\cdot\lambda_{n}^{d}\cdot\Psi\big(u-\mu_{t^{**}}\big)\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{3\sigma_{1}^{2}}\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\Psi\big(u-\mu_{t^{**}}\big)\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{3\sigma_{1}^{2}}\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}+\log|\mathcal{T}|+d\log\lambda_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\Psi\big(u-\mu_{t^{**}}\big)\delta_{n}^{2},

with the final inequality following because
\{g_{n}(\varepsilon_{0})\}^{2}\geq 6\sigma_{1}^{2}(\log|\mathcal{T}|+(d+2)\log\lambda_{n})
. Thus
\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1})]\leq C\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{**}})]\delta_{n}^{2}\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\cdot\sum_{t^{*}\in T^{*}}\bar{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]
. Combining the upper bounds on
\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{0})]
and
\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{1})]
yields the claim of Proposition
3
.

11
Proofs for Section
5

Section
5
contains four main results: Theorem
3
on the asymptotic conditional distribution of peaks; Theorem
4
on the asymptotic distribution of pivots; Corollary
1
on conditional coverage; and Theorem
5
which bounds the asymptotic PCMR. The proof of Theorem
3
is given in Sections
11.1
-
11.2
. The proof of Theorem
4
is given in Sections
11.3
-
11.10
. The proof of Corollary
1
is given in
11.11
. The proof of Theorem
5
is given in Section
11.12
.

11.1
Proof of Theorem
3

As mentioned in the main text, under the assumptions of Section
3.1
, as
\delta_{n}\to 0
the
\mathbb{P}
-probability of observing a unique peak
\hat{t}\in\widehat{T}
that consistently estimates
t^{*}
and has height
\widehat{Y}\in\mu_{t^{*}}\pm\Delta_{n}
tends to one. This is a consequence of the following stronger result.

Proposition 4
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
5
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large:

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{A}}\frac{|\mathbb{P}\big(N(\mathcal{A})=1,N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1\big)-\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{A})]|}{\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{A})]}\leq C\exp\big(-c\lambda_{n}^{2}\big),

(96)

where the supremum is over all measurable subsets
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}
.

The proof of Proposition
4
is given in Section
11.2
. Note that taking
u=-\infty,\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}
in Proposition
4
shows that

\mathbb{P}(N_{\mu_{t^{*}}\pm\Delta_{n}}(\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}})=1)\geq\mathbb{E}[N_{\mu_{t^{*}}\pm\Delta_{n}}(\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}))]\big(1-O(\exp(-\lambda_{n}^{2}))\big)\geq 1-O(\delta_{n}^{2}),

with the latter inequality following from Proposition
2
. In other words, each true peak
t^{*}
will be
\varepsilon_{n}
-consistently estimated by a unique observed peak
\hat{t}\in\widehat{T}
, with asymptotic probability one.

We now use Proposition
4
to derive (
29
), which upper bounds the relative error between
p(t,y)
and
\bar{p}(t,y)
. First, application of Proposition
4
with
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}
upper bounds
|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}=1)/\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]-1|
. A second application of Proposition
4
with
\mathcal{A}=B_{d}(t,r)\times[y-r,y+r]
then implies

\displaystyle\frac{\Big|\mathbb{P}(N(\mathcal{A}_{r})=1,N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1)/\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]-\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{A}_{r})]/\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\Big|}{E[N(\mathcal{A}_{r})]/\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]}\leq C\exp\big(-c\lambda_{n}^{2}\big).

(97)

Combining these two results and taking the limit as
r\to 0
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\Big|p(t,y)-\rho(t,y)/\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\Big|}{\rho(t,y)/\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]}\leq C\exp\big(-c\lambda_{n}^{2}\big).

(98)

The local expansion
\bar{p}(t,y)=\bar{\rho}(t,y)/\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]
, with the bound on relative error (
29
) following from (
98
), Theorem
1
and Proposition
2
. We do not give the specific calculations involved explicitly, because they are similar to the bookkeeping carried out explicitly in the proof of Theorem
1
. Similarly, the local expansion
\bar{p}(y)=\bar{\rho}(y)/\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]
, with the bound on relative error (
31
) following from (
98
), Lemma
8
and Proposition
2
.

The same is essentially also true for
\bar{p}(t|y)
and
\mathrm{Err}_{p}(t|y)
, but the details are slightly more complicated. Recall that in the proof of Lemma
8
, we introduced

\tilde{\rho}(y)=\frac{\sqrt{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)\det(-\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}})}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{1}{2}T_{01}^{\rho}(y)\Big).

Now additionally define

\displaystyle\tilde{p}(t^{*}+h|y):=\frac{\bar{\rho}(t^{*}+h,y)}{\tilde{\rho}(y)}=\sqrt{\frac{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}{(2\pi)^{d}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h\Big)\bigg(\frac{1+T_{10}^{\rho}(h)-\frac{1}{2}R_{30}^{\rho}(h)+T_{01}^{\rho}(y)+T_{21}^{\rho}(h,y)}{1+\frac{1}{2}T_{01}^{\rho}(y)}\bigg).

(99)

Inspecting the proof of Lemma
8
shows that
|\tilde{\rho}(y)-\bar{\rho}(y)|/\tilde{\rho}(y)\leq\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(y)
. Combining this observation with Lemma
8
, (
98
) and Theorem
1
implies that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large:

\frac{|\tilde{p}(t^{*}+h|y)-p(t^{*}+h|y)|}{\tilde{p}(t^{*}+h|y)}\leq C\big(\delta_{n}^{2}+\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(y)\big).

(100)

Thus
\tilde{p}(t|y)
is a second-order accurate local expansion of
p(t|y)
. An upper bound on the relative error between
\tilde{p}(t|y)
and
\bar{p}(t|y)
follows from Taylor expansion of several simple functions involving first-order terms in
\tilde{p}(t|y)
: for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1+T_{01}^{\rho}(y)}{1+\frac{1}{2}T_{01}^{\rho}(y)}-\Big(1+\frac{1}{2}T_{01}^{\rho}(y)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\{T_{01}^{\rho}(y)\}^{2}\leq C\cdot\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(h,y),

\displaystyle\frac{\Big|\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}-\sqrt{\det(\bar{G}_{t^{*}})}(1+\frac{1}{2}T_{01}^{\rho}(y))\Big|}{\sqrt{\det(\bar{G}_{t^{*}})}}

\displaystyle\leq C\{T_{01}^{\rho}(y)\}^{2}\leq C\cdot\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(h,y),

\displaystyle\Big|\exp(T_{21}^{\rho}(h,y))-(1+T_{21}^{\rho}(h,y))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\{T_{21}^{\rho}(h,y)\}^{2}\leq C\cdot\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(h,y),

and therefore

\frac{|\bar{p}(t^{*}+h|y)-\tilde{p}(t^{*}+h|y)|}{\tilde{p}(t^{*}+h|y)}\leq C\cdot\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(h,y).

(101)

Combining (
100
) and (
101
) yields (
33
).

11.2
Proof of Proposition
4

Our goal is to upper bound

\Big|\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{A})]-\mathbb{P}(N(\mathcal{A})=1,N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1)\Big|.

We begin with a deterministic upper bound:
N(\mathcal{A})-\mathbf{1}(N(\mathcal{A})=1,N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1)
– which is always non-negative – is at most

N(\mathcal{A})-\mathbf{1}(N(\mathcal{A})=1,N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1)\leq N(\mathcal{A})\cdot\mathbf{1}(N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})>1)\leq N(\mathcal{A})\cdot\mathbf{1}\bigg(\sup_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\max}(\nabla^{2}Y_{s})\geq 0\bigg).

Here we are using the fact that if the maximum eigenvalue of
\nabla^{2}Y_{s}
over
s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
is negative, then
Y
is strongly concave in
\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
and hence has at most one local maximum. Decomposing the Hessian
-\nabla^{2}Y_{s}=H_{s}^{t}(y)+R_{s}^{t}
– where we recall the projection/residual notation from (
49
) – allows us to further upper bound the final quantity on the right hand side by the counting process

N\Big((t,y)\in\mathcal{A}:\nabla Y_{t}=0,Y_{t}=y,\sup_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\max}(R_{s}^{t})\geq\inf_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\min}(H_{s}^{t}(y))\Big),

The results of Section
9.3
, specifically (
51
), further imply that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
large enough such that
\lambda_{n}\geq C\Delta_{n}
, for all
t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}},y\in\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}
:

\inf_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\min}(H_{s}^{t}(y))\geq\lambda_{\min}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})-C\Delta_{n}\geq\lambda_{n}-C\Delta_{n}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n},

Taking expectations, we conclude that

\Big|\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{A})]-\mathbb{P}(N(\mathcal{A})=1,N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1)\Big|\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[N\Big((t,y)\in\mathcal{A}:\nabla Y_{t}=0,Y_{t}=y,\sup_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\max}(R_{s}^{t})\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}\Big)\Big]

By the Kac-Rice formula, the expectation on the right hand side of the previous display is

\int_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(\nabla^{2}Y_{t}\prec 0,\sup_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\max}(R_{s}^{t})\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}\big)|Y_{t}=y,\nabla Y_{t}=0\Big]\cdot f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dy\,dt.

We upper bound this via Hölder’s inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(\nabla^{2}Y_{t}\prec 0,\sup_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\max}(R_{s}^{t})\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}\big)|Y_{t}=y,\nabla Y_{t}=0\Big]\cdot f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{A}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[|\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})|^{q_{1}}|Y_{t}=y,\nabla Y_{t}=0\Big]\Big\}^{1/q_{1}}\cdot\Big\{\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\max}(R_{s}^{t})\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}|Y_{t}=y,\nabla Y_{t}=0\Big)\Big\}^{1/q_{2}}\cdot f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dy\,dt,

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{A}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[|\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})|^{q_{1}}|Y_{t}=y,\nabla Y_{t}=0\Big]\Big\}^{1/q_{1}}\cdot\Big\{\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\max}(R_{s}^{t})\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}\Big)\Big\}^{1/q_{2}}\cdot f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\,dy\,dt,

where
q_{1}>1,q_{2}>1,1/q_{1}+1/q_{2}=1
are Hölder conjugates, and in the equality we have used the fact that
R_{s}^{t}
is independent of
(Y_{t},\nabla Y_{t})
. We proceed to upper bound each term in the previous display. In the proof of Lemma
1
, it was shown that for all
(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large,

\mathbb{E}\Big[|\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})|^{q_{1}}|Y_{t}=y,\nabla Y_{t}=0\Big]\Big\}^{1/q_{1}}\leq C(\lambda_{n}^{d}+1)\leq C|\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})|.

Lemma
2
implies that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large,

f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0)\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}}{2}-\frac{(t-t^{*})^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}(t-t^{*})}{2}\Big).

Finally, it is easy to see that (
52
) holds with
\xi_{n}
replaced by
\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}^{2}
. Taking
\eta=\sigma_{22}^{2}/8
in that equation yields

\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{s\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\max}(R_{s}^{t})\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}^{2}\Big)\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}}{8\sigma_{22}^{2}}\Big).

Thus, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[N(\mathcal{A})\big]-\mathbb{P}\big(N(\mathcal{A})=1,N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}}{8q_{2}\sigma_{22}^{2}}\Big)\int_{\mathcal{A}}|\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})|\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}}{2}-\frac{(t-t^{*})^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}(t-t^{*})}{2}\Big)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}}{8q_{2}\sigma_{22}^{2}}\Big)\int_{\mathcal{A}}\bar{\rho}(t,y)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\Big(-\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}}{8q_{2}\sigma_{22}^{2}}\Big)\int_{\mathcal{A}}\rho(t,y)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=C\exp(-c\lambda_{n}^{2})\cdot\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{A})],

which is exactly the claim of Proposition
4
.

11.3
Proof of Theorem
4
: deterministic limit of observed Hessian

In this section, we build on the results of Section
9.3
to show that under
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}
the relative error between
\widehat{H}
and
\bar{H}_{t^{*}}
converges to
0
in conditional probability, given
\{\hat{t}=t,\widehat{Y}=y\}
.

We begin by writing
\widehat{H}
in terms of the projection/residual decomposition defined in Section
9.3
:

\widehat{H}=H_{\hat{t}|\widehat{Y}}+\widehat{R},

where
\widehat{R}=R_{\hat{t}}
. The joint distribution of the triple
(\widehat{R},\hat{t},\widehat{Y})
under the law
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}
is continuous, and we denote its density by
p(R,t,y)
, and define the conditional density
p(R|t,y):=p(R,t,y)/p(t,y)
, where
p(t,y)
is the joint density of location and height defined in (
28
).

Now we recall the result of (
51
):

\sup_{(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\|H_{t|y}-\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|\leq C\Delta_{n}.

If additionally
R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}=B_{d\times d}(0,\xi_{n})\cap\mathbb{R}_{sym}^{d\times d}
then

\sup_{(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}},R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\frac{\|H_{t|y}+R-\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|}{\|\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|}\leq C\frac{(\Delta_{n}+\xi_{n})}{\lambda_{n}}\leq C\varepsilon_{n}.

(102)

It remains to upper bound the probability that
\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}^{c}
under
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}
and given
\{\hat{t}=t,\widehat{Y}=y\}
. This probability can be written as

\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}^{c}|\hat{t}=t,\widehat{Y}=y)=\lim_{r\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{P}(\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}^{c},N(\mathcal{A}_{r})=1)}{\mathbb{P}(N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1,N(\mathcal{A}_{r})=1)}.

Here
\mathcal{A}_{r}=B_{d}(t,r)\times[y-r,y+r]
. By Proposition
4
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large the denominator is at least
\frac{1}{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{A}_{r})]
for all
r>0
. On the other hand, the numerator is upper bounded by

\mathbb{E}\bigg[N\Big((t,y)\in\mathcal{A}_{r}:\nabla Y_{t}=0,Y_{t}=y,\nabla^{2}Y_{t}\prec 0,\|R_{t}\|>\xi_{n}\Big)\bigg]\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{A}_{r})],

with the inequality following by arguments similar to those used to prove Proposition
4
. As a result, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large

\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}^{c}|\hat{t}=t,\widehat{Y}=y)\leq C\delta_{n}^{2},

(103)

and in combination with (
102
) this implies

\sup_{(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\Big(\frac{\|\widehat{H}-\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|}{\|\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|}\geq C\varepsilon_{n}|\hat{t}=t,\widehat{Y}=y\Big)\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}.

11.4
Proof of Theorem
4
: pivot for height

We will work under the assumptions of Theorem
4
and establish (
35
). Lemma
9
shows that
\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(\widehat{Y})
is approximately pivotal.

Lemma 9
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4
,

\Big|\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(\widehat{Y})\leq\alpha\big)-\alpha\Big|\leq C\big(|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|\delta_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}\big):=\mathrm{Err}_{\bar{\mathbb{S}}}.

(104)

The proof of Lemma
9
is given in Section
11.5
. Lemma
10
bounds the difference between
\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}
and
\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}
on a high probability set.

Lemma 10
.

For all
(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}
and
R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
,

\Big|\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y,t,H_{t|y}+R)-\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y)\Big|\leq C(|\bar{u}-\mu_{t^{*}}|+\Delta_{n}+1)\varepsilon_{n}\delta_{n}:={\rm PlugIn}_{\mathbb{S}}.

(105)

The proof of Lemma
10
is given in Section
11.6
. Now we use Lemmas
9
and
10
to establish (
35
). Recall that the quantity we are interested in is

\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\Big(\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(\widehat{Y},\hat{t},\widehat{H})\leq\alpha\Big)=\int_{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{sym}^{d\times d}}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y,t,H_{t|y}+R)\leq\alpha\}\cdot p(R|t,y)\cdot p(t,y)\,dR\,dt\,dy.

We truncate this integral to the range at which the approximation of Lemma
10
applies; applying this lemma and then Lemma
9
gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\Big(\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(\widehat{Y},\hat{t},\widehat{H})\leq\alpha\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y,t,H_{t|y}+R)\leq\alpha\}\cdot p(R|t,y)\cdot p(t,y)\,dR\,dt\,dy

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\geq}\int_{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\mathbf{1}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y)\leq\alpha-{\rm PlugIn}_{\mathbb{S}})\cdot p(R|t,y)\cdot p(t,y)\,dR\,dt\,dy

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\mathbf{1}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y)\leq\alpha-{\rm PlugIn}_{\mathbb{S}})p(t,y)\,dt\,dy-\sup_{(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}^{c}}p(R|t,y)\,dR

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\geq}\alpha-{\rm PlugIn}_{\mathbb{S}}-\mathrm{Err}_{\bar{\mathbb{S}}}-\sup_{(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}^{c}}p(R|t,y)\,dR

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\geq}\alpha-{\rm PlugIn}_{\mathbb{S}}-\mathrm{Err}_{\bar{\mathbb{S}}}-C\delta_{n}^{2}

Above, (i) follows from Lemma
10
, (ii) follows from Lemma
9
, and (iii) follows from (
103
). A symmetric upper bound follows from essentially equivalent reasoning. Notice that
{\rm PlugIn}_{\mathbb{S}}+\mathrm{Err}_{\bar{\mathbb{S}}}+C\delta_{n}^{2}\leq C\cdot\mathrm{Err}_{\mathbb{S}}
. Thus, to establish (
35
) it remains to prove Lemma
9
and Lemma
10
.

11.5
Proof of Lemma
9

The quantity we are after is

\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(\widehat{Y})\leq\alpha)=\int_{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}\mathbf{1}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y)\leq\alpha)\cdot p(t,y)\,dt\,dy=\int_{\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}\mathbf{1}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y)\leq\alpha)\cdot p(y)\,dy.

Replacing
p(y)
by
\bar{p}(y)
, and adjusting the limits of integration, we have

\Big|\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(\widehat{Y})\leq\alpha)-\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y)\leq\alpha)\cdot\bar{p}(y)\,dy\Big|\leq\int_{\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}|p(y)-\bar{p}(y)|\,dy+\int_{(u,\infty)\setminus\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}\bar{p}(y)\,dy.

By definition

\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y)\leq\alpha)\cdot\bar{p}(y)\,dy=\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}^{-1}(\alpha))=\alpha,

and so it remains only to bound the error terms in the previous display. In the proof of Proposition
2
, it is shown that

\int_{\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\bar{\rho}(y)\,dy\leq C\cdot\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\quad\textrm{and}\quad\int_{(u,\infty)\setminus\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}\bar{\rho}(y)\,dy\leq C\cdot\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]\delta_{n}^{3}

Consequently, the error due to local expansion is at most

\int_{\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}|p(y)-\bar{p}(y)|\,dy\leq\int_{\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}\mathrm{Err}_{p}(y)\bar{p}(y)\,dy\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}\big(|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|+1\big)\bar{p}(y)\,dy\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}(|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|^{2}+1),

while the error due to truncation is at most

\int_{(u,\infty)\setminus\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}\bar{p}(y)\,dy=\int_{(u,\infty)\setminus\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}}\frac{\bar{\rho}(y)}{\bar{\mathbb{E}}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]}\,dy\leq C\delta_{n}^{3},

which is negligible by comparison.

11.6
Proof of Lemma
10

We find it convenient to introduce the following notation:

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle=\mu_{t^{*}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda)

\displaystyle E_{1}(t,y,R)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}((\{H_{t|y}+R\}^{-1}-\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1})\Lambda_{t})

\displaystyle E_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\Lambda_{t}-\Lambda\}),

so that the estimated survival function is

\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y,t,H_{t|y}+R)=\frac{\Psi(y-m-E_{1}(t,y,R)-E_{2}(t))}{\Psi(u-m-E_{1}(t,y,R)-E_{2}(t))}.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, the following bounds on the magnitude of
|E_{1}(t,y,R)|
and
|E_{2}(t)|
hold uniformly over
(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}},R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
:

\displaystyle|E_{1}(t,y,R)|\leq C\|\{H_{t|y}+R\}^{-1}-\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\|\cdot\|\Lambda_{t}\|\overset{(i)}{\leq}C\frac{\|H_{t|y}+R-\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|}{\|\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|^{2}}\|\Lambda_{t}\|\overset{(ii)}{\leq}C\varepsilon_{n}\delta_{n}.

where
(i)
follows from a first-order Taylor expansion of
A\mapsto A^{-1}
(stated in (
153
) for completeness), while (ii) follows from (
102
); and more simply

|E_{2}(t)|\leq\|\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\|\cdot\|\Lambda_{t}-\Lambda\|\leq C\delta_{n}\varepsilon_{n}.

An implication of the upper bounds on
|E_{1}(t,y,R)|
and
|E_{2}(t)|
is that uniformly over
(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}
and
R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
,
|y-m|\cdot(|E_{1}(t,y,R)|+|E_{2}(t)|)\to 0
and
|u-m|\cdot(|E_{1}(t,y,R)|+|E_{2}(t)|)\to 0
. We can therefore apply Lemma
14
to both numerator and denominator of
\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y,t,H_{t|y}+R)
, and conclude that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large

\displaystyle\frac{\Psi(y-m-E_{1}(t,y,R)-E_{2}(t))-\Psi(y-m)}{\Psi(y-m)}

\displaystyle\leq C((y-m)_{+}+1)\varepsilon_{n}\delta_{n},

(106)

\displaystyle\frac{\Psi(u-m-E_{1}(t,y,R)-E_{2}(t))-\Psi(u-m)}{\Psi(u-m)}

\displaystyle\leq C((u-m)_{+}+1)\varepsilon_{n}\delta_{n},

and therefore

\Big|\widehat{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y,t,H_{t}(y)+R)-\bar{\mathbb{S}}_{\mu}(y)\Big|\leq C((y-m)_{+}+(u-m)_{+}+1)\varepsilon_{n}\delta_{n}\leq C(\Delta_{n}+|\bar{u}-\mu_{t^{*}}|+1)\varepsilon_{n}\delta_{n}.

(107)

11.7
Proof of Theorem
4
: pivot for location

We work under the assumptions of Theorem
4
and establish (
36
). Consider the
studentized peak

\widehat{Z}=Z_{\hat{t}}(\widehat{Y},\widehat{R})=\widehat{F}(\hat{t}-t^{*}),\quad\textrm{where}\quad\widehat{F}=F_{\hat{t}}(\widehat{Y},\widehat{R})=\Lambda_{\hat{t}}^{-1/2}\widehat{H}=\Lambda_{\hat{t}}^{-1/2}(H_{\hat{t}|\widehat{Y}}+\widehat{R})

The Wald pivot is simply
\widehat{W}:=\widehat{W}_{t^{*}}(\hat{t})=\widehat{Z}^{\prime}\widehat{Z}
. We begin with a high-level outline of the derivation of (
36
). First we give a second-order accurate local expansion
\bar{p}(R|t,y)
of the conditional density of
\widehat{R}
given
\hat{t}=t,\widehat{Y}=y
. Then, we use
\bar{p}(R|t,y)
and Theorem
3
to derive an approximation to
p(t,R|y)
, the joint density of
(\widehat{t},\widehat{R})
given
\widehat{Y}=y
. We use this to deduce first a local expansion of the density of
\widehat{Z}
, and then a local expansion of the distribution of
\widehat{W}
. Finally, this limiting distribution of
\widehat{W}
is a generalized chi-squared distribution, that can be approximated by a
\chi_{d}^{2}
distribution – with loss of second-order accuracy – under the conditions of Theorem
4
.

Local expansion of density of Hessian.

As in the proof of Theorem
3
, we derive a local expansion of
p(R|t,y)
by first relating this conditional density to the intensity function of a relevant counting process. Concretely, arguments similar to the proof of Proposition
4
can be used to show that at any
R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}},(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}
,

\bigg|\frac{p(R,t,y)-\rho(R,t,y)/\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]}{\rho(R,t,y)/\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]}\bigg|\leq C\exp(-c\lambda_{n}^{2}).

where

\rho(R,t,y)=\det(H_{t|y}+R)\cdot\mathbf{1}(H_{t|y}+R\succ 0)\cdot f_{R_{t}}(R)\cdot f_{Y_{t}}(y)\cdot f_{\nabla Y_{t}}(0),

(108)

is the intensity function of a process that counts peaks of a given height, location, and residual Hessian; recall that we use
f_{R_{t}}(R)
to denote the density of
R_{t}
, which does not depend on
(Y_{t},\nabla Y_{t})
by construction.
In combination with (
98
) this implies

\bigg|\frac{p(R|t,y)-\rho(R,t,y)/\rho(t,y)}{\rho(R,t,y)/\rho(t,y)}\bigg|\leq C\exp(-c\lambda_{n}^{2}).

(109)

Thus we have related the density of
\widehat{R}|\hat{t},\widehat{Y}
under
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}
to the Palm distribution of
\widehat{R}
, which has density

\frac{\rho(R,t,y)}{\rho(t,y)}=\frac{\det(R+H_{t|y})\cdot\mathbf{1}(R+H_{t|y}\succ 0)}{\mathbb{E}[\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})\cdot\mathbf{1}(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t}\prec 0)|\nabla Y_{t}=0,Y_{t}=y]}\cdot f_{R_{t}}(R),

(110)

where
f_{R_{t}}(R)
is the Gaussian density

f_{R_{t}}(R)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\det(\Theta_{t})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R)\Big),

(111)

and
\Theta_{t}=\mathrm{Var}[{\rm vech}(R_{t})]
is invertible as a result of the condition
\tau_{2}^{2}>0
. We now derive an approximation to
p(R|t,y)
by local expansion of (
110
) about
t=t^{*},R=0
. We introduce the following notation for the first-order terms in this expansion:

\displaystyle T_{10}^{R}(h)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(h))

\displaystyle T_{12}^{R}(h,R)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(h)\}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R)

\displaystyle T_{01}^{R}(R)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}R).

Lemma 11
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4
, at any
t^{*}\in T^{*},h\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{n}),y\in\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}
and
R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
,

\bigg|\frac{p(R|t^{*}+h,y)-\bar{p}(R|t^{*}+h,y)}{\bar{p}(R|t^{*}+h,y)}\bigg|\leq C\Big(\|h\|^{2}+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\|h\|^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}\Big):=\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|h,y),

where

\bar{p}(R|t^{*}+h,y):=\Big(1+T_{10}^{R}(h)+T_{12}^{R}(h,R)+T_{01}^{R}(R)\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\cdot\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R)\Big).

(112)

Lemma
11
is proved in Section
11.8
.

Joint density of location and residual Hessian.

Lemma
11
and Theorem
3
can be used to compute an approximation to the joint density of
(\widehat{R},\hat{t})
given
\widehat{Y}
, the density of
\widehat{R}
given
\widehat{Y}
, and the density of
\hat{t}
given
(\widehat{R},\widehat{Y})
, which are respectively

p(R,t|y)=p(R|t,y)\cdot p(t|y),\quad p(R|y)=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}p(R,t|y)\,dt,\quad p(t|R,y)=\frac{p(R,t|y)}{p(R|y)}.

To more concisely denote the error involved in these expansions, let

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R,y)

\displaystyle:=C\big(|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|+\|R\|^{2}\big)\delta_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{\rm Trunc}}_{\rho}(\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}})

\displaystyle:=C\delta_{n}^{2}

Lemma 12
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4
, the following statements hold at any
t^{*}\in T^{*},h\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{n}),y\in\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}}
and
R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
:

•

The joint density of
(\widehat{R},\widehat{t})
given
\widehat{Y}
satisfies

\bigg|\frac{p(R,t^{*}+h|y)-\bar{p}(R|t^{*}+h,y)}{\bar{p}(R|t^{*}+h,y)}\bigg|\leq C\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{p}(h|y)\Big):=\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R,h|y),

where

\displaystyle\bar{p}(R,t^{*}+h|y):=

\displaystyle\Big(1+T_{10}^{R}(h)+T_{12}^{R}(h,R)+T_{01}^{R}(R)+T_{10}^{\rho}(h)-\frac{1}{2}T_{30}^{\rho}(h)\Big)

(113)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R)\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h\Big).

•

The conditional density of
\widehat{R}
given
\widehat{Y}
satisfies

\frac{|\bar{p}(R|y)-p(R|y)|}{\bar{p}(R|y)}\leq C\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R,y)+\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{\rm Trunc}}_{\rho}(\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}})\Big):=\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|y),

where

\bar{p}(R|y)=\Big(1+T_{01}^{R}(R)\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\cdot\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R)\Big).

•

The conditional density of
\hat{t}
given
\widehat{R},\widehat{Y}
satisfies

\frac{|\bar{p}(t^{*}+h|R,y)-p(t^{*}+h|R,y)|}{\bar{p}(t^{*}+h|R,y)}\leq C\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R,h|y)+\mathrm{Err}_{p}(h|y)\Big):=\mathrm{Err}_{p}(h|R,y),

where

\bar{p}(t^{*}+h|R,y):=\Big(1+T_{10}^{R}(h)+T_{12}^{R}(h,R)+T_{10}^{\rho}(h)-\frac{1}{2}T_{30}^{\rho}(h)\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h\Big).

Lemma
12
is proved in Section
11.9
.

Density of studentized peak.

Recall the studentized peak
\widehat{Z}=Z_{\hat{t}}(\widehat{R},\widehat{Y})
. Lemma
12
, combined with a change of variables, yields an approximation to the joint density of
(\widehat{Z},\widehat{R})
given
\widehat{Y}
, the conditional density of
\widehat{Z}
given
(\widehat{R},\widehat{Y})
, and the conditional density of
\widehat{Z}
given
\widehat{Y}
and
\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
. We introduce some notation to compactly denote these approximations. We write
F_{t^{*}}=F_{t^{*}}(y,0)
and
\dot{F}_{t^{*}}
for
\nabla_{t}F_{t}(y,0)|_{t=t^{*}}
. We let
\tilde{L}_{t}
be the differential of
\Lambda_{t}^{-1/2}
, meaning any array in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}
that satisfies
\{L_{t}(h)\}^{\prime}\Lambda_{t}^{-1/2}+\Lambda_{t}^{-1/2}\{L_{t}(h)\}=-\Lambda_{t}^{-1}\{\dot{\Lambda}_{t}(h)\}\Lambda_{t}^{-1}
for all
h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We write
L_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}
for the array that swaps the second and third indices of
\tilde{L}_{t}
, so that
[L_{t}]_{ijk}=[\tilde{L}_{t}]_{ikj}
, and write
\bar{L}_{t}=L_{t}+\tilde{L}_{t}
. Finally, we denote the first-order terms in these expansions by

\displaystyle T_{01}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)

\displaystyle=T_{10}^{R}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)+T_{10}^{\rho}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)-2\cdot\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}\{\dot{H}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\})-\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\bar{L}_{t^{*}}(H_{t^{*}}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}\big(\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\big)+\mathrm{tr}\big(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{H}_{t^{*}|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\big)+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}\big(\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{\Lambda}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\big)

\displaystyle-2\cdot\mathrm{tr}\big(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\big)-\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\bar{L}_{t^{*}}(H_{t^{*}}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\})

\displaystyle T_{30}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}T_{30}^{\rho}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)-z^{\prime}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z,F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)

\displaystyle T_{21}^{p_{Z|R}}(z,R)

\displaystyle=z^{\prime}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1})^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}RF_{t^{*}}^{-1}z

\displaystyle T_{12}^{p_{Z|R}}(z,R)

\displaystyle=T_{12}^{R}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z,R)=\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R),

\displaystyle T_{01}^{p_{Z|R}}(R)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R),

and for the relative error incurred by the expansion

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z,\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}|y)

\displaystyle:=C\delta_{n}^{2}\big(1+\|z\|^{6}\big)\big(1+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|\big)

\displaystyle{\rm Trunc}_{p}(\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}})

\displaystyle:=C\delta_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{\rm Trunc}}_{p}(\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}})

\displaystyle:=C\delta_{n}^{2.5}.

Finally, let
\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}(y)=\{Z_{t^{*}+h}(0,y):2h\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{n})\}
.

Lemma 13
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4
, the following statements hold at any
y\in\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}},z\in\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}(y)
and
R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
:

•

The conditional density of
\widehat{Z}
given
\widehat{R}=R,\widehat{Y}=y
satisfies

\Big|\frac{p(z|R,y)-\bar{p}(z|R,y)}{\bar{p}(z|R,y)}\Big|\leq C\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z|R,y)+\delta_{n}^{2}(1+\|z\|^{6})(1+\|R\|^{2})\Big):=\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z|R,y),

(114)

where

\displaystyle\bar{p}(z|R,y)

\displaystyle:=\big(1+T_{01}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)+T_{30}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)+T_{21}^{p_{Z|R}}(z,R)+T_{12}^{p_{Z|R}}(z,R)+T_{01}^{p_{Z|R}}(R)\big)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}\det(F)}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\Big).

•

The joint density of
\widehat{Z}
and
\widehat{R}
given
\widehat{Y}=y
satisfies

\Big|\frac{p(z,R|y)-\bar{p}(z,R|y)}{\bar{p}(z,R|y)}\Big|\leq C\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z|R,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|y)\Big):=\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z,R|y)

where

\displaystyle\bar{p}(z,R|y)

\displaystyle:=\big(1+T_{01}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)+T_{30}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)+T_{21}^{p_{Z|R}}(z,R)+T_{12}^{p_{Z|R}}(z,R)+T_{01}^{p_{Z|R}}(R)+T_{01}^{R}(R)\big)

(115)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}\det(F_{t^{*}})}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\cdot\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R)\Big).

•

The conditional density of
\widehat{Z}
given
\widehat{Y}=y
and
\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
satisfies

\Big|\frac{p(z|y)-\bar{p}(z|y)}{\bar{p}(z|y)}\Big|\leq C\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z,\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}|y)+{\rm Trunc}_{p}(\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}})+\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{\rm Trunc}}_{p}(\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}})\Big):=\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z|y),

(116)

where

\bar{p}(z|y)=\Big(1+T_{01}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)+T_{30}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}\big(\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\}\big)\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}\det(F_{t^{*}})}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\Big).

Lemma
13
is proved in Section
11.10
. To be perfectly clear, the density
p(z|y)
conditions both on
N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})=1
and on
\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
, i.e.:

p(z|y):=\frac{1}{\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}})}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}p(z,R|y)\,dR.

Distribution of studentized Wald pivot.

We now turn to the conditional distribution of the studentized Wald pivot
\widehat{W}
, given the selection event
\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}
and conditional on the height
\widehat{Y}
. To additionally condition on the high-probability event
\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
, we sandwich

\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{W}\leq q,\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}|\widehat{Y}=y)\leq\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{W}\leq q|\widehat{Y}=y)\leq\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{W}\leq q,\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}|\widehat{Y}=y)+\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{R}\notin\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}|\widehat{Y}=y).

We note that
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{R}\notin\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}|\widehat{Y}=y)\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}
and

\Big|\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{W}\leq q,\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}|\widehat{Y}=y)-\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{W}\leq q|\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}},\widehat{Y}=y)\Big|\leq C\delta_{n}^{2},

both by (
103
). Since
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{W}\leq q|\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}},\widehat{Y}=y)=\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}p(z|y)\,dz
, we have succeeded in showing that

\bigg|\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{W}\leq q|\widehat{Y}=y)-\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}p(z|y)\,dz\bigg|\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}.

Notice that
B_{d}(0,q)\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large. We can therefore apply Lemma
13
to conclude that

\Big|\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}p(z|y)\,dz-\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}\bar{p}(z|y)\,dz\Big|\leq\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z|y)\bar{p}(z|y)\,dz.

(117)

Since each of the first-order terms in the local expansion
\bar{p}(z|y)
is an odd function of
z
, they vanish when integrated over
B_{d}(0,q)
, leaving us with

\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}\bar{p}(z|y)\,dz=\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}\det(F_{t^{*}})}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\Big)\,dz.

(118)

In other words
\widehat{W}
has a limiting generalized chi-squared distribution, up to second-order relative error

\displaystyle C\delta_{n}^{2}+\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z|y)\bar{p}(z|y)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\delta_{n}^{2}(1+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|)\Big).

(119)

Derivation of (
36
).

Equation (
118
) gives a second-order accurate approximation to
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{W}\leq q|\widehat{Y}=y)
. We now expand this approximation in
y
, around
y=\mu_{t^{*}}
, to obtain the ultimate claim (
36
), valid when
(\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}})\delta_{n}\to 0,y\in\bar{u}_{t^{*}}\pm\Delta_{n}
and therefore
(y-\mu_{t^{*}})\delta_{n}\to 0
. We begin by simplifying the integrand of (
118
). We have that the precision matrix

F_{t^{*}}^{-1}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}=\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}(-\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}})H_{t^{*}|y}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}=I-(y-\mu_{t^{*}})\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}H_{t^{*}|y}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}.

Applying the matrix perturbation bound (
153
) allows us to bound
\|H_{t^{*}|y}^{-1}-\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|\leq C|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|\delta_{n}^{2}
, and in turn implies

\|F_{t^{*}}^{-1}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}-\Big(I-(y-\mu_{t^{*}})\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}\Big)\|\leq C\Big(|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}||y-\mu_{t^{*}}|\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

Then a subsequent Taylor expansion of
\exp(x)
around
x=0
– noting that the magnitude of the first-order term in the previous expression is
O((y-\mu_{t^{*}})\delta_{n})
– implies

\Big\|\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\Big)-\Big(1+\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})}{2}z^{\prime}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}z\Big)\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}z^{\prime}z\Big)\Big\|\leq C\Big((y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}||y-\mu_{t^{*}}|\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

Similarly,

\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\det(F_{t^{*}})}=\sqrt{\frac{\det(-\nabla^{2}\mu_{t^{*}})}{\det(H_{t^{*}|y})}}=\sqrt{\det(I-(y-\mu_{t^{*}})H_{t^{*}|y}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}})}.

(120)

Using a first-order Taylor expansion of
\det(I+E)
about
E=0
, as recorded in (
154
), and again applying the matrix perturbation bound (
153
) to upper bound
\|H_{t^{*}|y}^{-1}-\bar{H}_{t^{*}}\|
, we have

\Big\|\sqrt{\det(I-(y-\mu_{t^{*}})H_{t^{*}|y}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}})}-\Big(1-\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}})\Big)\Big\|\leq C\Big((y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+|y-\mu_{t^{*}}||y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

Putting these two approximations together yields the following approximation to the integrand of (
118
):

\bigg|\phi_{0,F_{t^{*}}^{-1}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}}(z)-\Big(1-\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}})+\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})}{2}z^{\prime}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}z\Big)\phi_{0,I}(z)\bigg|\leq C\Big((y-\mu_{t^{*}})^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+|y-\mu_{t^{*}}||y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

(121)

Integrating this approximation over
B_{d}(0,q)
gives

\int_{B_{d}(0,q)}\Big(1-\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}})+\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})}{2}z^{\prime}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{1/2}z\Big)\phi_{0,I}(z)\,dz=\chi_{d}^{2}(q)-A\cdot\frac{(y-\mu_{t^{*}})}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}),

which equals
\mathbb{P}(\widehat{W}\leq q|\widehat{Y}=y)
up to the second-order error terms in (
119
) and (
121
), which are at most
C\delta_{n}^{2}(1+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|^{2})
.

11.8
Proof of Lemma
11

The density of the Palm distribution of
R_{t}|\{\hat{t}=t,\widehat{Y}=y\}
is given in (
110
); up to an exponential error term, this agrees with the density of
R_{t}||\{\hat{t}=t,\widehat{Y}=y\}
under
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}
as shown in (
109
). For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
large enough and
(t,y)\in\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}},R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
, the matrix
H_{t^{*}|y}+R
will be positive definite, and then the Palm density is simply the product of a Gaussian density and a determinant term, divided by a normalizing constant:

\displaystyle p(R|t,y)=\frac{\det(R+H_{t|y})}{\mathbb{E}[\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})\cdot\mathbf{1}(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t}\succ 0)|\nabla Y_{t}=0,Y_{t}=y]}f_{R_{t}}(R).

(122)

We separately approximate the Gaussian density, and the determinant term and normalizing constant. Throughout we write
r={\rm vech}(R)
and
h=t-t^{*}
for notational convenience.

Gaussian density of residual.

Recall that the Gaussian density of the residual Hessian
R_{t}
is

f_{R_{t}}(R)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\det(\Theta_{t})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}r^{\prime}\Theta_{t}^{-1}r\Big),

A first-order Taylor expansion of
\Theta_{t}^{-1}
about
t=t^{*}
implies

\Big|r^{\prime}\Theta_{t}^{-1}r-r^{\prime}(\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}-\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(h)\}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1})r\Big|\leq C\|h\|^{2}\|R\|^{2},

while first-order Taylor expansion of
\{\det(\Theta_{t})\}^{-1/2}
about
t=t^{*}
implies

\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\det(\Theta_{t})}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}(1-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(h))\Big|\leq C\|h\|^{2};

in both cases we are using (
2
) which implies that
\lambda_{\min}(\Theta_{t})>\tau_{2}^{2}>0
. These combine to yield an approximation to the log of
f_{R_{t}}(R)
. From here, arguments similar to those of Section
9.6
yield

\frac{|f_{R_{t}}(R)-\bar{f}_{R_{t}}(R)|}{\bar{f}_{R_{t}}(R)}\leq C\Big(\|R\|^{2}\|h\|^{2}+\|h\|^{2}\Big)

where

\bar{f}_{R_{t}}(R):=\frac{\Big(1-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}\big(\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(h)\big)+\frac{1}{2}r^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\dot{\Theta}_{t^{*}}(h)\}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}r\Big)}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}r^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}r\Big).

(123)

Determinant term and normalizing constant.

Recall the result of (
58
) in Section
9.3
:

\displaystyle\frac{\Big|\det(H_{t|y}+R)-\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})\big(1+T_{10}^{\det}(h)+T_{01}^{\det}(y)+\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}R)\big)\Big|}{\det(\bar{H}_{t^{*}})}\leq C\Big(\|h\|^{2}+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

Combined with Lemma
1
, this implies

\Big|\frac{\det(H_{t|y}+R)}{\mathbb{E}[\det(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t})\cdot\mathbf{1}(-\nabla^{2}Y_{t}\succ 0)|\nabla Y_{t}=0,Y_{t}=y]}-\big(1+\mathrm{tr}(\bar{H}_{t^{*}}^{-1}R)\big)\Big|\leq C\Big(\|h\|^{2}+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}\Big).

(124)

Combining (
123
) and (
124
) give the approximation
\bar{p}(R|t,y)
to the claimed order of accuracy.

11.9
Proof of Lemma
12

Throughout this proof we denote
r={\rm vech}(R)
and
t=t^{*}+h
.

11.9.1
Joint density of residual Hessian and location

Lemma
11
and Theorem
3
imply that for all
n
sufficiently large:

\frac{\Big|p(R,t|y)-\bar{p}(R|t,y)\bar{p}(t|y)\Big|}{\bar{p}(R|t,y)\bar{p}(t|y)}\leq C\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|t,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{p}(t|y)\Big).

The product
\bar{p}(R|t,y)\bar{p}(t|y)
is

\displaystyle\bar{p}(R|t,y)\bar{p}(t|y)

\displaystyle=\Big(1+T_{10}^{\rho}(h)-\frac{1}{2}T_{30}^{\rho}(h)\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(1+T_{10}^{R}(h)+T_{12}^{R}(h,R)+T_{01}^{R}(R)\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\cdot\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}r^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}r\Big).

Ignoring the product of all first-order terms in the above equation incurs at most a second-order error, meaning precisely:

\frac{|\bar{p}(R|t,y)\bar{p}(t|y)-\bar{p}(R,t|y)|}{\bar{p}(R,t|y)}\leq C\Big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|t,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{p}(h|y)\Big),

and establishing (
113
). We give an explicit bound on the magnitude of the error term
\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R,h|y)
in terms of
(\delta_{n},h,y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}},R)
, for future reference:

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{p}(h|y)

(125)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{\rho}(h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]})

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{\det}(h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{Y}(h,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{\nabla Y}(h)+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\lambda_{n}^{2}\|h\|^{4}+\mathrm{Err}_{\mathbb{E}[N(\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}})]})

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|h\|^{2}+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\delta_{n}^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\|h\|^{2}+\delta_{n}^{2}+\big(|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|\big)\lambda_{n}\|h\|^{3}+\lambda_{n}^{2}\|h\|^{4}+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}\lambda_{n}^{2}\|h\|^{4}\Big).

11.9.2
Density of residual Hessian.

Let

\tilde{p}(R,t^{*}+h|y)=\Big(1+T_{01}^{R}(R)\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R)\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h\Big).

The difference between
\tilde{p}(R,t^{*}+h|y)
and
\bar{p}(R,t^{*}+h|y)
is that the former omits first-order terms that are linear in
h
. The conditional density of the residual Hessian is
p(R|y)=\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}p(R,t|y)\,dt
. We expand this as the sum of a main term and three error terms:

p(R|y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{p}(R,t|y)\,dh-\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\tilde{p}(R,t|y)\,dt+\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\bar{p}(R,t|y)-\tilde{p}(R,t|y)\,dt+\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}{p}(R,t|y)-\bar{p}(R,t|y)\,dt.

The main term above
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{p}(R,t|y)\,dt=\bar{p}(R|y)
. The second error term
\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\bar{p}(R,t|y)-\tilde{p}(R,t|y)\,dt=0
, since the integral of any first-order term that is linear in
h
over
\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
vanishes. It remains to bound the two sources of error, due to local expansion and truncation.

Error due to local expansion.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large,

\bar{p}(R,t|y)\leq C\cdot\bar{p}(R|y)\cdot\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h\Big).

Combined with (
113
), this implies that the error due to local expansion is at most

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\big|{p}(R,t|y)-\bar{p}(R,t|y)\big|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R,h|y)\cdot\bar{p}(R,t|y)\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\bar{p}(R|y)\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}\int_{B(0,\varepsilon_{n})}\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R,h|y)\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h\Big)\,dh.

Inserting the upper bound on
\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R,h|y)
from (
125
) into the previous display, and integrating over
h\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{n})
, shows that the error due to local expansion is at most

C\cdot\bar{p}(R|y)\Big(|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|+\|R\|^{2}+1)\delta_{n}^{2}.

Truncation error.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large,

\tilde{p}(R,t|y)\leq C\cdot\bar{p}(R|y)\cdot\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h\Big).

Moreover, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large,
\varepsilon_{n}\geq\{\lambda_{\min}(G_{t^{*}|y})\}^{-1}\sqrt{5\log\lambda_{n}}
. Thus
\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\tilde{p}(R,t|y)\,dt
is at most

C\cdot\bar{p}(R|y)\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(t-t^{*})^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}(t-t^{*})\Big)\,dt\leq C\cdot\bar{p}(R|y)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{d}(0,\sqrt{5\log\lambda_{n}})}\exp\Big(-\frac{\|z\|^{2}}{2}\Big)\,dz\leq C\delta_{n}^{2},

with the final inequality following by (
150
).

11.9.3
Conditional density of location

Finally, the local expansion
\bar{p}(t|R,y)
to the conditional density of
\hat{t}|\{\widehat{R}=R,\widehat{Y}=y\}
is obtained by dividing
\bar{p}(t,R|y)
by
\bar{p}(R|y)
. Proper bookkeeping shows that the ratio of first-order terms can be ignored, at the cost of a second-order error term that is absorbed into
\mathrm{Err}_{p}(t|R,y)
.

11.10
Proof of Lemma
13

We begin with some calculus preliminaries before deriving the local expansions of Lemma
13
. For ease of reading, we suppress all notational dependence on
y
, writing
F_{t}(R)=F_{t}(y,R)
,
Z_{t}(R)=Z_{t}(y,R)
and
H_{t}=H_{t|y}
.

11.10.1
Calculus preliminaries.

Change of variables.

The Jacobian of the mapping
t\mapsto Z_{t}(R)
is given by

\dot{Z}_{t}(R)=F_{t}(R)+L_{t}\big((H_{t}+R)h\big)+\Lambda_{t}^{-1/2}\{\dot{H}_{t}(h)\}

and we recall that
[L_{t}]_{ijk}=[\tilde{L}_{t}]_{ikj}
, and
\tilde{L}_{t}
is the differential of
\Lambda_{t}^{-1/2}
, meaning it is any array in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}
that satisfies
\{L_{t}(h)\}^{\prime}\Lambda_{t}^{-1/2}+\Lambda_{t}^{-1/2}\{L_{t}(h)\}=-\Lambda_{t}^{-1}\{\dot{\Lambda}_{t}(h)\}\Lambda_{t}^{-1}
for all
h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Note that
D_{t}
is not the same as
\dot{F}_{t}
. We will use the fact that
\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|L_{t}(v)\|\leq C\|v\|
for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, as a result of (
2
).

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large and any
y\in\mathcal{I}_{t^{*}},R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
, the minimum eigenvalue of
\dot{Z}_{t}(R)
is uniformly bounded away from
0
over all
t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
: the minimum eigenvalue of
F_{t}(R)
is at least

\inf_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\lambda_{\min}(F_{t}(R))\geq\inf_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\Lambda_{t})}}\lambda_{\min}(H_{t}+R)\geq c(\lambda_{n}-\xi_{n})\geq c\lambda_{n},

while the norm of
\dot{F}_{t}(R)(t-t^{*})
is at most

\sup_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\|\dot{F}_{t}(R)(t-t^{*})\|\leq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\|A_{t}(t-t^{*})\|+C\|\dot{H}_{t}(t-t^{*})\|\leq C(\lambda_{n}+\xi_{n})\varepsilon_{n}\leq C\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n},

and therefore for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large:

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\|\dot{Z}_{t}(R)\|\geq\inf_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\|F_{t}(R)\|-\sup_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}}\|\dot{F}_{t}(R)(t-t^{*})\|\geq c\lambda_{n}(1-C\varepsilon_{n})\geq c\lambda_{n}.

We conclude that the vector field
t\mapsto Z_{t}(R)
is strongly monotonic: for all
s,t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
,

(Z_{s}(R)-Z_{t}(R))^{\prime}(s-t)\geq c\lambda_{n}\|s-t\|^{2},

and hence
t\mapsto Z_{t}(R)
is injective over
\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
. The change of variables formula for one-to-one mappings therefore gives the density of
\widehat{Z}|\widehat{R},\widehat{Y}
: letting
\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}(R):=\{Z_{t}(R):t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}\}
, we have that

p(z|R,y)=\frac{p(T_{z}(R)|R,y)}{|\det(\dot{Z}_{T_{z}(R)}(R))|},\quad\textrm{for $z\in\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}(R)$,}

(126)

where
T_{z}(R)
is the unique point
t\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
satisfying
Z_{T_{z}(R)}(R)=z
.

Taylor approximation of
T_{z}(R)
.

We now provide a local expansion of the inverse
T_{z}(R)
around
z=0,R=0
by inverting a local expansion of
Z_{t}(R)
around
t=t^{*},R=0
. The first-order Taylor expansion of
F_{t}(R)
around
t=t^{*},R=0
is

F_{t^{*}}+\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(t-t^{*})+\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R,

where we recall that
F_{t^{*}}=F_{t^{*}}(0)=\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}H_{t^{*}}
and
\dot{F}_{t^{*}}=\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(0)
. Applying (
56
) and
\|L_{t}(v)\|\leq C\|v\|
, we conclude that

\Big\|F_{t}(R)-F_{t^{*}}-\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(t-t^{*})-\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R\Big\|\leq C\lambda_{n}\|t-t^{*}\|^{2}.

Consequently, the first-order local expansion of
Z_{t}(R)
around
t=t^{*},R=0
is

F_{t^{*}}(t-t^{*})+\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(t-t^{*},t-t^{*})+\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R(t-t^{*}),

with error

\|Z_{t}(R)-F_{t^{*}}(t-t^{*})-\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(t-t^{*},t-t^{*})-\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R(t-t^{*})\|\leq C(\lambda_{n}\|t-t^{*}\|^{3}+\|R\|\|t-t^{*}\|).

Now we “invert” this expansion. To begin, evaluating
Z_{t}(R)
at
t=T_{z}(R)
and applying the above bound:

\displaystyle\Big\|z-F_{t^{*}}h_{z}(R)-\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(h_{z}(R),h_{z}(R))-\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}Rh_{z}(R)\Big\|\leq C(\lambda_{n}\|h_{z}(R)\|^{3}+\|R\|\cdot\|h_{z}(R)\|),

(127)

where
h_{z}(R)=T_{z}(R)-t^{*}
. Substituting
F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z
for
h_{z}(R)
in the first-order terms in (
127
), and observing that
\|h_{z}(R)\|\leq C\delta_{n}\|z\|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|z-F_{t^{*}}h_{z}(R)-\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z,F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)-\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\lambda_{n}\|h_{z}(R)\|^{3}+\|R\|\cdot\|h_{z}(R)\|^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\cdot\|h_{z}(R)\|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\Big(\|z\|^{3}+\|R\|\cdot\|z\|^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\|z\|\Big).

The first inequality above follows since
\|F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z-h_{z}(R)\|\leq C\|h_{z}(R)\|^{2}
by (
127
), and the second inequality follows since
\|h_{z}(R)\|\leq C\delta_{n}\|z\|
.
Finally, application of
F_{t^{*}}^{-1}
gives the first-order expansion to
h_{z}(R)
:

\displaystyle\Big\|h_{z}(R)-\bar{h}_{z}(R)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}^{3}\Big(\|z\|^{3}+\|R\|\|z\|^{2}+\|R\|^{2}\|z\|\Big):=\mathrm{Err}_{h}(z,R),

(128)

\displaystyle\bar{h}_{z}(R)

\displaystyle:=F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z-F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z,F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)-F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}RF_{t^{*}}^{-1}z

The magnitude of the first-order terms in this expansion are at most

\|F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z,F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\|\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\|z\|^{3},\quad\|F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}RF_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\|\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\|R\|\|z\|.

11.10.2
Conditional distribution of studentized peak given residual Hessian.

We now derive (
114
), which gives a local expansion of the conditional density
p(z|R,y)
, by separately providing approximations to the density term and determinant term in (
126
).

Density term.

Lemma
12
implies that the relative error between
p(T_{z}(R)|R,y)
and

\bar{p}(T_{z}(R)|R,y)=\Big(1+T_{10}^{R}(h_{z}(R))+T_{12}^{R}(h_{z}(R),R)+T_{10}^{\rho}(h_{z}(R))-\frac{1}{2}T_{30}^{\rho}(h_{z}(R))\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(h_{z}(R))^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}h_{z}(R)\Big),

is at most

\displaystyle\frac{|p(T_{z}(R)|R,y)-\bar{p}(T_{z}(R)|R,y)|}{\bar{p}(T_{z}(R)|R,y)}\leq\mathrm{Err}_{p}(h_{z}(R)|R,y)\leq C\cdot\mathrm{Err}_{p}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z|R,y),

with the last inequality following because
\|h_{z}(R)\|\leq C\|F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\|
, while
\mathrm{Err}_{p}(h|R,y)
is increasing in
\|h\|
. Substituting
\bar{h}_{z}(R)
for
h_{z}(R)
in the above expression incurs error that can be bounded above using (
128
); doing this, Taylor expanding
\exp(1+x)
around
x=0
, and disregarding second-order terms, we conclude that the relative error between
\bar{p}(T_{z}(R)|R,y)
and

\displaystyle\tilde{p}(z|R,y)

\displaystyle=\Big(1+T_{10}^{R}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)+T_{12}^{R}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z,R)+T_{10}^{\rho}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)-\frac{1}{2}T_{30}^{\rho}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)+

\displaystyle(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}\big(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z,F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)+F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}RF_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\big)\Big)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}}\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\Big)

is at most

\frac{|\bar{p}(T_{z}(R)|R,y)-\tilde{p}(z|R,y)|}{\tilde{p}(z|R,y)}\leq C\Big((1+\|R\|^{2})\cdot\mathrm{Err}_{h}(z,R)+\delta_{n}^{2}\big(1+\|z\|^{6}\big)\big(1+\|R\|^{2}\big)\Big)\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\big(1+\|z\|^{6}\big)\big(1+\|R\|^{2}\big).

For future reference, we note that

\mathrm{Err}_{p}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z|R,y)\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\big(1+\|z\|^{4}\big)\big(1+\|R\|^{2}\big)\big(1+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|\big),

Determinant term.

A first order Taylor expansion of
\dot{Z}_{t}(R)
about
t=t^{*},R=0
gives

\Big\|\dot{Z}_{t}(R)-F_{t^{*}}(h)-\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(h)-\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R-L_{t^{*}}H_{t^{*}}(h)-\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}\{\dot{H}_{t^{*}}(h)\}\big\|\leq C\Big(\lambda_{n}\|h\|^{2}+\|R\|\|h\|\Big).

(129)

As
\dot{F}_{t^{*}}(h)=\tilde{L}_{t^{*}}(H_{t^{*}}h)+\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}\{\dot{H}_{t^{*}}(h)
, we can just as easily write the first-order Taylor expansion as

F_{t^{*}}+2\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}\{\dot{H}_{t^{*}}(h)\}+\bar{L}_{t^{*}}(H_{t^{*}}h)+\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R.

From this, we see that the determinant term can be locally expanded as

\displaystyle\Big|\det\big(\dot{Z}_{t}(R)\big)-\det(F_{t^{*}})\big(1+2\cdot\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}\{\dot{H}_{t^{*}}(h)\})+\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\bar{L}_{t^{*}}(H_{t^{*}}h)\})+\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R)\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\det(F_{t^{*}})(\lambda_{n}\|h\|^{3}+\delta_{n}\|R\|\cdot\|h\|).

Evaluating this at
t=T_{z}(R)
, substituting
\bar{h}_{z}(R)
for
h_{z}(R)
, and disregarding second-order terms:

\displaystyle\Big|\det(\dot{Z}_{T_{z}(R)}(R))-\det(F_{t^{*}})\big(1+2\cdot\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}\{\dot{H}_{t^{*}}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\})+\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\{\bar{L}_{t^{*}}(H_{t^{*}}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)\})+\mathrm{tr}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}\Lambda_{t^{*}}^{-1/2}R)\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\det(F_{t^{*}})\delta_{n}^{2}\Big(\|z\|^{3}+\|R\|\cdot\|z\|\Big).

Notice that this can further be upper bounded by
C\det(F_{t^{*}})\delta_{n}^{2}(1+\|z\|^{6})(1+\|R\|^{2})
. Combined our analyses of the density term and determinant term yield the claimed result (
114
).

11.10.3
Joint distribution of studentized peak, residual Hessian.

The joint density of
\widehat{Z},\widehat{R}
given
\widehat{Y}=y
is

p(z,R|y)=p(z|R,y)\cdot p(R|y).

Equation (
114
) gives an approximation to
p(z|R,y)
, while Lemma
12
gives an approximation to
p(R|y)
. Combined, these results imply (
115
). The relative error incurred by these approximations is at most

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z,R|y)

\displaystyle=C\big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z|R,y)+\mathrm{Err}_{p}(R|y)\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\mathrm{Err}_{p}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z|R,y)+\delta_{n}^{2}(1+\|z\|^{6})(1+\|R\|^{2})+\delta_{n}^{2}(|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|+\|R\|^{2})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\big((1+\|z\|^{4})(1+\|R\|^{2})(1+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|)+(1+\|z\|^{6})(1+\|R\|^{2})+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|+\|R\|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\big((1+\|z\|^{6})(1+\|R\|^{2})(1+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|)\big).

11.10.4
Validity in
\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}
.

So far we have proven the validity of the local expansions
\bar{p}(z|R,y)
and
\bar{p}(z|y)
at points
z\in\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}(R)
. We now show that for
n
sufficiently large
\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}(R)
for all
R\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
. By definition
z\in\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}
means that
h_{z}(0)=F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\leq\varepsilon_{n}/2
. Application of (
127
) then implies that for all
n
sufficiently large

\|h_{z}(R)\|\leq\|F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\|+\|F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z-h_{z}(R)\|\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}+C\|h_{z}(R)\|^{2}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}+C\delta_{n}^{2}\|z\|^{2}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}+C\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{n};

in the second-to-last inequality we have used the fact that
\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}\subseteq B(0,\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n})
. Thus
T_{z}(R)\in\mathcal{B}_{t^{*}}
, implying that
z\in\mathcal{Z}_{t^{*}}(R)
.

11.10.5
Distribution of studentized peak.

Let

\displaystyle\tilde{p}(z,R|y)

\displaystyle:=\big(1+T_{01}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)+T_{30}^{p_{Z|R}}(z)+T_{12}^{p_{Z|R}}(z,R)\big))

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sqrt{\det(G_{t^{*}|y})}}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d}}\det(F_{t^{*}})}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z)^{\prime}G_{t^{*}|y}F_{t^{*}}^{-1}z\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{d(d+1)/2}\cdot\det(\Theta_{t^{*}})}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R)\Big).

The difference between
\tilde{p}(z,R|y)
and
\bar{p}(z,R|y)
is that the former omits all first-order terms that are linear in
R
. We decompose the density of
\widehat{Z}
given
\widehat{Y}=y
into the sum of a main term and four error terms:

\displaystyle p(z|y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}})}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}p(z,R|y)\,dR

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{sym}^{d\times d}}\tilde{p}(z,R|y)\,dR-\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\tilde{p}(z,R|y)\,dR+\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\bar{p}(z,R|y)-\tilde{p}(z,R|y)\,dR+\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\bar{p}(z,R|y)-p(z,R|y)\,dR+

\displaystyle\Big(1-\frac{1}{\mathbb{Q}^{\mathcal{S}_{t^{*}}}(\widehat{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}})}\Big)\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}p(z,R|y)\,dR.

The main term above is
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{sym}^{d\times d}}\tilde{p}(z,R|y)\,dR=\bar{p}(z|y)
. The second error term above is
\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\bar{p}(z,R|y)-\tilde{p}(z,R|y)\,dR=0
, since
\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
is symmetric about the origin and so the integral of any odd function in
R
vanishes over
\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}
. It remains to bound the three sources of error, due to local expansion, undoing the effects of truncation, and using the wrong normalizing constant.

Error due to local expansion.

Applying (
115
), we can upper bound the error due to local expansion:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\bar{p}(z,R|y)-p(z,R|y)\,dR

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z,R|y)\cdot\bar{p}(z,R|y)\,dR

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z,R|y)\cdot\bar{p}(R|z,y)\cdot\bar{p}(z|y)\,dR

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}}(1+\|z\|^{6})(1+\|R\|^{2})(1+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|)\cdot\bar{p}(R|z,y)\cdot\bar{p}(z|y)\,dR

\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{n}^{2}(1+\|z\|^{6})(1+|y-\bar{u}_{t^{*}}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{t^{*}}-\mu_{t^{*}}|)\bar{p}(z|y):=\mathrm{Err}_{p}(z,\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}|y)\cdot\bar{p}(z|y).

Error due to truncation.

In bounding the truncation error, observe that
\sigma_{22}^{2}\geq\lambda_{\min}(\Theta_{t^{*}})
and therefore
\xi_{n}\geq\{\lambda_{\min}(\Theta_{t^{*}})\}^{1/2}\sqrt{6\log\lambda_{n}}
. Therefore,

\int_{\mathcal{R}_{t^{*}}^{c}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2}{\rm vech}(R)^{\prime}\Theta_{t^{*}}^{-1}{\rm vech}(R))\,dR\leq\int_{B_{d\times d}(0,\sqrt{6\log\lambda_{n}})^{c}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2}\|{\rm vech}(R)\|^{2})\,dR\leq C\delta_{n}^{2.5},

(130)
